**python-jose → PyJWT (2026-09-29, user อนุมัติ install + แก้ `/backend/`) — product envelope, ไม่ใช่ contribution:**
- แก้ 3 ไฟล์: `backend/auth.py` (`import jwt`, `except jwt.PyJWTError`), `backend/tests/test_auth.py` (`import jwt`), `backend/requirements.txt` (`python-jose[cryptography]` → `PyJWT`). ติดตั้ง PyJWT 2.15.1 ใน `backend/venv`. `encode`/`decode` signature เดิม ไม่แก้ logic
- Verify: `python -m unittest discover -s tests` 442 tests OK (skipped=1); `-k test_auth` 34/34 (รวม expired/bad-sub/no-jti/rotation); token ที่ mint ด้วย jose ยัง decode ผ่าน `_decode_token` ใหม่ และกลับกัน → session เดิมของ user ไม่โดนเตะ. **รัน uvicorn จริง + Postgres dev DB แล้ว (2026-09-29):** register 201 → login 200 → `/me` 200 → refresh 200 → `/me` 200; `/me` ไม่มี cookie 401, token ปลอม 401, logout 200 แล้ว `/me` 401 (ทิ้ง user ทดสอบ `pyjwt_check_*@example.com` id 54 ไว้ใน dev DB)
- uninstall `python-jose` + `ecdsa` ออกจาก `backend/venv` แล้ว (user อนุมัติ; `pip check` สะอาด, tests 442 OK หลังถอน). **คง `rsa`, `pyasn1`, `pyasn1_modules` ไว้** — `google-auth` (ผ่าน `google-genai` ที่ Gemini ใช้) ต้องการ ไม่ใช่ของ jose

**Local LLM Generator + Gemini Critic — Milestone 1 implemented AND benchmarked for real (2026-09-14 code, 2026-09-18 real benchmark run, merged to `main` at `acd61c81`) — not a 4th contribution:**
- Advisor asked to evaluate a local trained model replacing Gemini, or local-primary + Gemini-verifies. Brainstormed with user + external review (Codex). Design: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-local-llm-generator-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-local-llm-generator-design.md); Plan: [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-local-llm-generator-milestone1.md`](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-local-llm-generator-milestone1.md); ledger: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-14-local-llm-generator-milestone1/progress.md`
- **Maps into existing contribution slots, no 4th contribution opened:** local model (Qwen Instruct, candidate sizes 4B / 7B–9B, no fine-tuning yet) becomes primary generator; Gemini retained as critic — specifically the already-planned "LLM hidden-allergen layer" (layer 3 of Allergen Validation contribution, **not built this milestone**, deferred). **Correction from initial framing (still holds):** the Critic-Feedback Loop's blind-retry→feedback fix is **not** open work — it already shipped 2026-07 (commit `0949f304`). `MEMORY.md`'s "Blind-retry ไม่ใช่ critic-feedback loop" entry has been updated to note the fix and flag this exact gotcha (a real, billed Gemini API call almost recurred twice more during this milestone's TDD — see below).
- **What was built (additive only, zero existing files modified except `README.md`):** `backend/generators/{base,prompts,gemini_generator,local_generator}.py` (pluggable `RecipeGenerator` interface, shared prompt builder with a few-shot path for the local model, `GeminiGenerator` as a new parallel twin of `call_agentic_llm()` — **`main.py`/`call_agentic_llm()` itself is completely untouched, the live endpoint's behavior has not changed at all**), `backend/eval/{metrics.py,run_benchmark.py,fixtures/generator_eval_cases.json}` (benchmark harness reusing the real `validate_recipe()` pipeline read-only, comparing Qwen 4B / Qwen 7B-9B / Gemini on schema validity, explicit-allergen avoidance, ingredient hallucination, and latency — **not yet run for real numbers, no vLLM server exists yet**), plus 5 new test files (27 new tests). Full backend suite: **148/148 passing.**
- **Real, recurring risk class found and fixed 3 times in this milestone:** any test/script that falls back to `os.getenv("GEMINI_API_KEY")` when a key isn't explicitly passed will make a REAL, BILLED call to production Gemini in a dev environment whose `.env` has a real key — not a hypothetical, it actually happened once during Task 3's TDD. Fixed via `@patch.dict(os.environ, ...)` isolation (Tasks 3, 4) and a corrected `run_benchmark.py` docstring + amended plan-doc smoke-test instructions (final review, since the plan's own Task 6 Step 4 as originally written would have triggered it a 3rd time). Logged as a standing gotcha in `MEMORY.md` for any future generator/API-calling test in this codebase.
- **Final whole-branch review (opus) caught 3 Important findings, 1 fix wave, all verified addressed:** (1) an eval fixture (`shrimp_allergy_should_avoid_shrimp_and_shrimp_paste`) set `"allergy": "กุ้ง"` with no trigger keyword, so `main.py`'s `check_allergy()` silently skipped it entirely — the fixture could never fail the check it was named for, regardless of what a generator produced. **This is the exact "Circularity trap"/"Terminology overclaiming" failure class already logged in `MEMORY.md`** — a metric that claims to measure something it structurally cannot. Fixed: `"allergy": "แพ้กุ้ง"` (verified via direct trace of `main.py`'s real keyword list and allergen map that the check now actually fires). (2) The Gemini-vs-Qwen benchmark comparison silently varies both model AND prompt (Gemini uses `include_example=False`, Qwen uses `include_example=True` — intentional per design, but undocumented) — now an explicit code comment; the 4B-vs-7B comparison is the clean, prompt-matched one. (3) `run_benchmark.py`'s docstring inaccurately implied running with no credentials was always safe — corrected.
- **Rulings made (parked, not fixed, with reasoning):** `check_ingredient_hallucination`'s substring-match has real false negatives on Thai homophone-adjacent names (e.g. "ไก่" ⊂ "ไข่ไก่") — plan-mandated design, accepted as a known heuristic limitation for this milestone (proper Thai tokenization is out of scope), now explicitly documented in the function's own docstring so it's never silently over-trusted. `summarize_run` computes mean latency only, though the plan's prose once said "mean/median" — the plan's own code block only ever specified mean; kept as shipped, revisit if the eval set grows past hand-built n=3.
- **1.5-month milestone scope (โครงงานพิเศษ 1) — what this delivers:** generator abstraction interface (`generate(..., feedback=None)`, swappable Gemini↔local via config) + `LocalLLMGenerator` via vLLM + prompt/schema engineering + a benchmark harness. **Still deferred, unchanged:** rewiring the live `/api/generate-recipe-text` endpoint (separate, future, separately-confirmed `/backend/main.py`-touching task); Gemini critic layer wiring; `LocalLLMGenerator` feedback-injection parity with `GeminiGenerator`; Evaluations 2 (critic effectiveness) and 3 (blind-retry vs feedback-loop, empirically).
- **Terminology guardrail (unchanged from design):** base-menu lookup via existing `base_recipes` table is "Base Recipe Retrieval / Template Grounding", **not RAG** — reserved for the separate Personalization RAG (pgvector). Gemini-checking-its-own-Gemini-output would be "second-pass semantic critique"; only Qwen→Gemini would be "cross-model verification using a different model family/provider" — neither claim applies yet since the critic layer isn't built.
- **Merged:** `worktree-local-llm-generator-milestone1` branch merged to `main` (`acd61c81`), branch+worktree deleted. No `/backend/` file was modified without the explicit checkpoint confirmation the user gave before Task 1 (14 new files, listed and confirmed).

**Real benchmark run (2026-09-18) — RunPod Secure Cloud, real GPU, real money spent (~$0.86 total across the whole session, well under the $10 top-up):**
- **Model-size correction (NO MAGIC catch before spending real money):** the design/plan's placeholder "Qwen 4B / 7B–9B" doesn't exist — Qwen2.5 has no 4B or 9B (only 0.5B/1.5B/3B/7B/14B/32B/72B); verified via web search before provisioning anything. Used **`Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B`** and **`Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B`** instead (real, confirmed on Hugging Face + official vLLM recipes) — closer to the original intent than the Qwen3 fallback (4B/8B) considered first. Fixed the hardcoded model strings in `backend/eval/run_benchmark.py`'s `candidates` dict to match (small, confirmed backend edit).
- **GPU/infra lessons (each cost real trial-and-error before landing on a working setup):**
  1. RunPod GPU stock and driver version vary **per physical host**, not per GPU model name — two separate L4 pods landed on old-driver (CUDA 12.8) hosts before `minCudaVersion: "13.0"` on `create-pod` forced a CUDA-13-capable host. vLLM's own compiled extensions (`vllm._C_stable_libtorch`) require `libcudart.so.13` regardless of which torch build is paired with it — pinning torch to cu128 via `uv --torch-backend=cu128` did **not** fix this; only landing on an actual CUDA-13-driver host did.
  2. `runpodctl pod create` (no template volume) defaults to a 20GB container disk with no separate persistent volume — filled up mid-install. Needed explicit `--container-disk-in-gb 60`. Pods created via the MCP `create-pod` tool with a `templateId` get a 50GB mount automatically; plain `runpodctl` CLI creation does not — the two paths aren't equivalent.
  3. `Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B` in bf16 alone uses ~18.2GB VRAM, leaving too little headroom for KV cache on a 24GB card even at a reduced `--max-model-len` — needed `--quantization fp8` to actually fit and serve. The "fits comfortably on a 24GB GPU" claim from vLLM's own recipe docs implicitly assumed quantization, not stated explicitly.
  4. One GPU type (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell MIG 1g.24gb slice) failed entirely with `RuntimeError: FlashInfer requires GPUs with sm75 or higher` — a MIG-slice/attention-backend incompatibility, not a driver/CUDA version issue. Switched to a plain (non-MIG) A100 80GB instead of debugging further, given time cost.
  5. `ssh <host> '<cmd1>; sleep N; <cmd2>'` (a `sleep` in the same SSH invocation as other commands) can drop the channel (exit 255) per `runpod-usage`'s own documented gotcha — confirmed empirically once. Split into separate SSH calls instead.
- **Model behavior lesson (would have silently broken every local-model benchmark row):** `Qwen/Qwen3.5-*` are hybrid thinking/non-thinking models — by default they emit reasoning into a separate `reasoning` field and leave `message.content` **null** until reasoning finishes, which often doesn't happen within any reasonable token budget (500 tokens of reasoning on a two-line Thai greeting, still unfinished). Fixed by adding `"chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": false}` to `LocalLLMGenerator`'s request body (`backend/generators/local_generator.py`, confirmed backend edit) — without this, `content` is null and every case scores as an unparseable failure, not a real quality signal.
- **Results — 4B tested 3 times (2 different GPUs), 9B tested once, Gemini baseline re-run each time (real billed calls, small):**

  | Candidate | Schema validity | Hallucination rate | Mean latency |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Gemini (baseline) | 0.33–0.67 (varies run to run, no fixed seed) | 0.0 | ~4–8.7s |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.0–0.33 (consistent across 3 runs) | 0.67–1.0 (consistent) | ~1.6–14.7s (hardware-dependent) |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.67 (1 run only) | 0.33 | ~18.9s |

  4B's poor result is **repeated evidence, not a single-run fluke** — 3 independent runs on 2 different GPU types all showed low validity and a **repeatable qualitative failure**: non-Thai tokens (Greek, Chinese script) leaking into generated Thai recipe text. 9B has only one real run so far and needs a repeat before being trusted the same way.
- **Decision: not fine-tuning yet.** Two of the plan's own preconditions for fine-tuning aren't met: (1) benchmark evidence is still thin for 9B (n=3, one run) and 4B's one-run-worse-than-Gemini gap doesn't yet prove prompting can't close it; (2) no real failure cases from actual usage exist — only the 3 hand-built synthetic fixtures. Recommended next steps instead: try fixing 4B via prompting (explicit "Thai only" instruction, stronger few-shot) before writing it off; get a second 9B run; then wire the Gemini critic layer + `LocalLLMGenerator` feedback parity + Evaluations 2/3 (all still deferred from Milestone 1) before revisiting training with real usage-derived failure cases.
- **Prompt-fix attempt (2026-09-18, 4th real 4B run, A100 SXM):** added a local-model-only rule to `build_recipe_prompt()` (`backend/generators/prompts.py`, gated behind `include_example=True` so it never touches `GeminiGenerator`'s production-matched prompt): fields that must be Thai may still use English where the schema already calls for it (recipe_name/diet_tags), but must never contain a third script (Greek/Chinese/Korean/Cyrillic) with no reason to be there. Result: **schema_validity_rate and hallucination_rate came back identical to the prior run (0.33 / 0.67)** — the garbled tokens that did appear looked milder (`"ข่า 1 โ SDL"` vs. earlier full non-Latin words), but n=3 is too small to call that a real improvement over noise. One case also newly errored after a 48s hang rather than returning a parseable failure — a new failure mode, not previously seen. **Conclusion: this specific prompt tweak did not clearly fix 4B.** Leaning further toward "4B not viable for this task without deeper work (better few-shot, or accept 9B as the local candidate)" — still not a final call given the small sample.
- **9B retest (2026-09-18, 2nd real 9B run, fresh L4 pod, same fp8/3072-context config):** schema_validity_rate came back **identical** to the first run (0.667/0.667 — a genuinely consistent signal across 2 runs). But hallucination_rate swung from 0.33 to **1.0**, and one case showed a new, more severe failure: the model dumped raw English/Hindi-script reasoning text directly into the `unknown_ingredients` field (`"मे triggered by rule or context? No, vegetables not listed..."`) — **despite `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` being set**, meaning that flag does not fully suppress reasoning leakage into `content` for this model, at least not reliably under fp8 quantization. **Revised conclusion: 9B is not clearly better than 4B after all** — schema validity looks consistent and reasonably good, but 9B has its own real, repeatable-looking failure mode (reasoning-text leakage) distinct from 4B's (garbled foreign-script tokens). Neither candidate is currently trustworthy without further fixing; picking between them isn't resolved by this milestone's evidence. Total spend across both benchmark sessions (2026-09-18): **$1.31** of the $10 top-up.
- **Metric-analysis correction (2026-09-19, before the guided-decoding fix below):** initially misread `"ข้าวสวย"`/`"กระเทียมสับ"` flags as `_PANTRY_STAPLES` false positives and proposed widening that allowlist — **wrong, retracted without editing code.** Checked `backend/eval/fixtures/generator_eval_cases.json` directly: neither case's ingredient list includes rice or garlic, and the production prompt's rule 6 explicitly restricts the model to the user's ingredients plus exactly 7 named staples (no rice, no garlic) — a deliberate restriction tied to this project's presence-only fridge design (inventory accuracy, not "what a Thai kitchen typically has"). Widening the metric's allowlist would have made it *more permissive than the system's own actual rule*, hiding genuine rule violations rather than correctly flagging them. `_PANTRY_STAPLES` was left unchanged.
- **Root-cause split and fix: guided JSON decoding (2026-09-19), verified on a live vLLM server before landing in code.** Traced the real benchmark failures to two different causes: (1) decoding-time — the model emitting off-schema scripts (Greek/Arabic/Tamil/Chinese) or leaking raw reasoning text into a JSON string value, neither of which the loose `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` prevents; (2) instruction-following — the model adding ingredients outside what it was told it may use (a semantic constraint, not something decoding-level constraints can enforce). Tested `response_format: {"type": "json_schema", ...}` with per-field regex `pattern`s (English-only for `recipe_name`/`diet_tags`, Thai+Latin+digits for the Thai-required fields) directly against a live vLLM server, iterating on the exact pattern before writing any code:
  - A plain allowed-character-class pattern **reliably blocked all off-schema scripts** across 2 separate live tests — the severe failure mode from every prior run.
  - A `(?=.*[Thai])` lookahead ("field must contain at least one Thai character") was also tried and found **not reliably enforced** — the identical pattern applied to two sibling fields in the same schema produced inconsistent results (one field honored it, one didn't) — this vLLM version's constrained-decoding backend does not reliably support lookahead assertions in JSON-schema `pattern`. Deliberately not used in the shipped fix.
  - Shipped the character-class-only version to `backend/generators/local_generator.py` (`_RECIPE_JSON_SCHEMA`, confirmed backend edit, 148/148 tests still passing).
- **Full benchmark re-run with the guided-schema fix (2026-09-19, fresh L4 pod):**

  | Candidate | Schema validity (before → after guided JSON) | Hallucination rate (before → after) | Mean latency |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Gemini (baseline, unaffected by this fix) | 0.33–0.67 | 0.0 | ~2.3–4.6s |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.0–0.33 → **1.0** | 0.67–1.0 → **0.33** | ~13.5s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.67 (unchanged — now consistent across **3** separate runs) | 0.33/1.0/1.0 | ~19.6s |

  4B went from consistently the worst performer to a perfect 3/3 schema-validity run, with its one remaining flagged case a minor typo (`"น้ำมน"`, a truncated `"น้ำมัน"`) rather than a foreign-script failure. 9B's schema validity has now held at exactly 0.667 across three independent runs — a genuinely solid signal — and its severe failure modes (reasoning-leak, foreign scripts) did not recur under the guided schema; its remaining hallucination flags are the *legitimate* "added an ingredient the user doesn't have" violations described above, not decoding corruption.
- **Revised final conclusion for this session's work:** most of what looked like "the local model just isn't good enough" was actually a **fixable decoding-control problem**, not a knowledge/capability gap — exactly the outcome the "try cheaper interventions before fine-tuning" reasoning predicted. **4B now looks like the more practical candidate** (perfect validity, faster, cheaper to serve) contrary to every earlier session's leaning toward 9B. The one remaining real gap — models adding ingredients beyond what they were given — is a genuine instruction-following question decoding constraints structurally cannot fix; it's the most defensible candidate for a future fine-tuning case, but still only backed by n=3 synthetic fixtures, not real usage failure cases, so the plan's fine-tuning precondition still isn't met. Total spend across all benchmark sessions (2026-09-18/19): **$1.55** of the $10 top-up.
- **Retest round confirming the guided-schema fix is stable, not a fluke (2026-09-19, fresh L4 pod, results re-verified by parsing the saved JSON files directly rather than from memory before writing this):**

  | Model | Run | Schema validity | Hallucination rate |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 1st guided run | 1.000 | 0.333 |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 2nd guided run (retest) | **1.000** | 0.333 |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 1st guided run | 0.667 | 1.000 |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 2nd guided run (retest) | **1.000** | 0.667 |

  **4B: perfect schema validity 2/2 guided runs** — the most confidently-good result of this entire milestone, and now the more practical candidate (faster, cheaper, most reliable). **9B: improved from 0.667 to 1.000 on retest** (4 runs total across the whole session: 0.667/0.667/0.667/1.000) — still trending positive under the guided-schema fix, just with fewer confirming runs than 4B. Neither model's remaining hallucination flags (in this round) are decoding corruption anymore — they're the legitimate "added an ingredient beyond what was given" rule violations already described above, plus one interesting edge case: `"นํ้าเปล่าเล็กน้อย"` was flagged as unknown despite `น้ำเปล่า` being an allowed staple — likely a Thai Unicode normalization mismatch (precomposed vs. combining-mark forms of น้ำ), not a real hallucination; not yet fixed, flagged for later.

- **Safety-relevant finding, verified by parsing every saved benchmark run programmatically (not estimated from memory) — production-relevant, not just a benchmark curiosity: Gemini fails the shrimp-allergy fixture (`shrimp_allergy_should_avoid_shrimp_and_shrimp_paste`) in 8 of 9 runs across this entire session**, every time for the same reason (`Allergy violation: พบ 'กุ้ง' ในสูตร` — it included shrimp despite the user's stated allergy). By contrast, filtering to only the runs where each local model was actually the one being served (excluding runs where a benchmark row errored out because a different model was running on the pod at the time): **Qwen3.5-4B passed this same case 4 of 5 times**, and **Qwen3.5-9B passed it 3 of 4 times** — and in both local models' one miss each, the failure reason was unrelated to allergy (oil-less stir-fry; malformed instructions), not a missed allergen. **This is a real production concern, not just benchmark noise: `backend/main.py`'s live `/api/generate-recipe-text` endpoint calls Gemini today, for real users, right now** — this specific, repeatable allergy-avoidance failure is exactly what the Allergen Validation contribution's deterministic rule+KG check (`validate_recipe`'s allergy stage) exists to catch downstream, and per this data, does catch every time in this benchmark (that's why Gemini's `schema_validity_rate` scores low here) — but it means the *generator* itself is not reliably respecting an explicit allergy instruction, and every one of those Gemini failures cost a wasted retry cycle. Caveat: this is one fixture/one allergen (shrimp/กะปิ) repeated across runs, not a diverse allergy test set — the signal is strong and consistent for this specific scenario, but should not be generalized to "Gemini is unsafe for all allergies" without testing more allergen scenarios.
  - Total spend across the entire local-LLM-generator benchmark effort (2026-09-18/19, all pods): **$1.66** of the $10 top-up.
- All RunPod pods deleted after use; no pod left running. `backend/.env`'s `LOCAL_LLM_BASE_URL` still points at a now-deleted pod's URL (stale, harmless — next real run needs a fresh pod and a fresh URL there anyway).
- **`run_benchmark.py` extended to save the full generated `recipe` object per case (commit `f9e1fc11`), then a fresh capture run made to let the user personally inspect real recipe text (2026-09-19), not just metrics** — `benchmark-4b-full-recipe.json`/`benchmark-9b-full-recipe.json` (scratchpad, not committed — benchmark output, not project source). Personally verified the Gemini shrimp-allergy finding above directly from real recipe text: Gemini's output still lists `กะปิ` (shrimp paste) as an ingredient and only appends a warning telling the user to check the label themselves, rather than substituting it — confirms the logged finding is real, not a metric artifact.
- **False alarm, retracted: no encoding corruption in the benchmark JSON files.** While inspecting the new full-recipe files via `Bash` + Python `print()` (piped through Git Bash on Windows), Thai text displayed as mojibake (`????`-style boxes) — initially misdiagnosed as a file-save encoding bug and almost logged as one. **Wrong.** Re-read the same files with the `Read` tool (no shell/console in the path) and the text was correct throughout. Root cause: Git Bash's console codepage mangles UTF-8 on **display** via `print()`/stdout piping on this Windows setup — the files on disk were never corrupted. **Correct behavior going forward: never diagnose Thai/non-ASCII text problems in a saved file via `Bash`+Python `print()` on this machine — use the `Read` tool, or if a script must inspect text, dump `[hex(ord(ch)) for ch in s]` to a file and check codepoints directly (bypasses console rendering entirely).** Logged to `MEMORY.md`.
- **Real finding upgraded from the retest round's parked note (line 148 above): the allergy-avoidance metric has a bigger gap than "Unicode normalization noise" — and it's a live `/backend/main.py` production bug, not just an eval-side metric limitation.** Re-inspecting the corrected (non-corrupted) 9B full-recipe file found a `shrimp_allergy_should_avoid_shrimp_and_shrimp_paste` case that scored `valid: true, reason: null` while the recipe still contained `กะปิ` (shrimp paste) as a plain ingredient with no hedge/warning text at all — unlike the Gemini case, which only got caught because its warning sentence happened to contain the literal word "กุ้ง". Traced it: `eval/metrics.py`'s `check_schema_and_constraints` calls `main.py`'s real `validate_recipe()` → `check_allergy()` read-only — so this is the exact same allergy check the live `/api/generate-recipe-text` endpoint runs for real users today. `ALLERGEN_MAP["shrimp"]["blocks"]` was `["shrimp", "prawn", "กุ้ง"]` — a literal substring match on the allergen word itself, no mapping from a known derived-ingredient name (`กะปิ`) to the allergen it's made from.
  - **Fixed as an interim patch (2026-09-19, commit `b1fd6293`, confirmed `/backend/main.py` edit): added `"กะปิ"` to the shrimp `blocks` list.** Verified directly (not just via test count): `check_allergy()` on `{"adjusted_ingredients": ["หมูสับ 200 กรัม", "กะปิ 1 ช้อนโต๊ะ"]}` with `{"allergy": "แพ้กุ้ง"}` now returns a violation (previously passed). 148/148 backend tests still passing.
  - **Explicitly not the real fix — do not conflate the two.** This project's Allergen Validation contribution design (line 33-34 above) already planned a proper Knowledge Graph layer (transitive derived-ingredient detection, e.g. `กะปิ→กุ้ง`, real node/edge/traversal — not a flat tag list) specifically for this class of gap; it is not built yet. The one-line patch above only covers the single entry this benchmark happened to surface (`กะปิ`) — it does not generalize to `น้ำปลากุ้ง`, `ผงชูรสกุ้ง`, or any other derived ingredient not manually added to the list. Applied now because it's a real, live safety gap affecting real users with an unclear KG timeline, not because it substitutes for the KG contribution — **never report this patch as "Allergen Validation Knowledge Graph implemented."**
- Same 9B file (verified via `Read` tool, confirmed not a display artifact) also showed the model inventing ingredients absent from the case's input list in 3/3 cases (`ข้าวสวย`, `น้ำมะนาว`/`ซอยหอมแดง`, `กระเทียมสับ`) and, separately, occasionally generating a malformed/nonsense Thai word (`"ไข่ง่า"` instead of `"ไข่ไก่"`, `"เปร็กรากาด"`) — confirmed via raw Unicode codepoint dump (`[hex(ord(ch)) ...]`, bypassing console rendering) that these are genuine valid-Thai-character sequences forming non-words, not corrupted bytes. Both are real generator-quality signals distinct from the guided-JSON decoding fix (which only constrains *script*, not spelling or instruction-following) — consistent with the already-logged "remaining hallucination flags are legitimate instruction-following gaps" conclusion, just with a starker example.
- **Independent recapture run (2026-09-19, fresh L4 pod `uab47ct8vfxmbo`, `$0.49/hr`, same fp8/3072-context 9B config) — done specifically to (a) confirm the retracted "encoding bug" theory with a clean re-run and (b) get the new `input` field into a real capture.** Inspected every result exclusively via `Read`/`Grep` this time, never `Bash`+`print()`, per the newly-logged MEMORY.md gotcha. Result: **byte-for-byte identical output to the previous (already-corrected, non-corrupted) 9B capture** — same latencies (15.813s/20.859s/21.672s), same `recipe_name`s, same malformed words (`"ไข่ง่า"`, `"เปร็กรากาด"`), same `กะปิ`-without-warning shrimp case. This is expected (`LocalLLMGenerator` calls vLLM at temperature 0 / deterministic) and doubles as independent confirmation: the earlier file was never corrupted, and the malformed-word / missed-derived-allergen findings are reproducible, not one-off sampling noise. 4B recapture (schema validity 1.0, hallucination 0.33) also matched prior guided-schema numbers. Both new files (`benchmark-4b-recapture.json`, `benchmark-9b-recapture.json`, scratchpad, not committed) now carry the `input` field (real per-case `ingredients`/`user_prefs`/`base_recipe`, not a template) alongside `recipe`, so any future finding can be verified against exactly what was fed in without opening the fixtures file separately. Pod deleted immediately after, confirmed via `list-pods` (empty). Total additional spend: well under $0.50 (one L4-hour).
- **Eval fixtures expanded 3 → 8 cases (2026-09-19, commit `70424c02`, confirmed `/backend/` edit) — the precondition both the user and this session had agreed on before seriously considering fine-tuning.** Scoped narrowly per the Hard Rule against opening a 4th contribution: only exercises allergens `ALLERGEN_MAP` already supports (peanut, milk, egg, gluten, a second peanut-derived case), no new allergen categories, no new code paths. Each new case's expected `check_allergy()` outcome was **verified empirically by calling it directly before committing**, not assumed:
  - `peanut_allergy_should_avoid_peanut`, `egg_allergy_should_avoid_egg`: straightforward true-positive violations.
  - `milk_allergy_coconut_milk_should_not_false_flag`: false-positive trap (`กะทิ`/coconut milk is not dairy) — verified it correctly passes (`check_allergy` → `("OK", True)`).
  - `gluten_allergy_soy_sauce_known_gap` (`ซีอิ๊วขาว`), `peanut_allergy_satay_sauce_known_gap` (`น้ำจิ้มสะเต๊ะ`): known-gap cases mirroring the `กะปิ` finding — verified both slip through `check_allergy()` undetected, same class of gap the planned Allergen Validation Knowledge Graph exists to close. **First attempt at a second case used `น้ำปลากุ้ง` (fish sauce made with shrimp) as a shrimp-derived known-gap analog — wrong, caught by direct verification before committing: it contains the literal substring `กุ้ง` so `check_allergy()` correctly blocks it, not a gap.** Replaced with the peanut-satay-sauce case instead.
  - 148/148 backend tests still passing after the expansion.
- **First real benchmark run against the expanded 8-case set (2026-09-19, fresh L4 pod `j8sawvtp8xg7wj`, `$0.49/hr`) — surfaced 2 new real `check_allergy()` false-positive bugs and a large quality drop for both local models vs. the old n=3 set:**

  | Candidate | Schema validity (n=3 → n=8) | Hallucination rate (n=3 → n=8) | Mean latency |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Gemini (run paired w/ 4B) | — | 0.625 | 0.0 | 3.1s |
  | Gemini (run paired w/ 9B) | — | 0.875 | 0.125 | 2.9s |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 1.0 → **0.625** | 0.33 → **0.75** | 15.0s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.667–1.0 → **0.375** | 0.33–1.0 → 0.75 | 21.3s |

  **Both local models score notably worse on the wider set — real evidence the n=3 numbers were an optimistic small-sample read, exactly the risk expanding the eval set was meant to catch.** But the raw numbers above overstate the drop somewhat: **2 of the "failures" turned out to be metric/check false positives, not real generator failures**, found by reading the actual `reason` strings and recipe text (not assumed):
  1. **`ALLERGEN_MAP["peanut"]` and `["soy"]` both listed the generic word `"ถั่ว"` as a trigger** — so a user stating `"แพ้ถั่ว"` (peanut allergy, the common colloquial meaning) also got checked against soy's blocks. Gemini's peanut case (run paired w/ 4B) was wrongly blocked: `"Allergy violation: พบ 'ซีอิ๊ว' ในสูตร (ผู้ใช้แพ้ soy)"` — the recipe had soy sauce, not peanuts, and the user never said they're allergic to soy. **Fixed (2026-09-19, commit `9f02089c`, confirmed `/backend/main.py` edit): removed `"ถั่ว"` from soy's triggers, kept under peanut only.** Verified directly: soy-sauce-only recipe under peanut allergy now passes; real peanut and explicit-soy (`"ถั่วเหลือง"`) cases still correctly block. 148/148 tests still passing.
  2. **Substring match on the full ingredients text also catches negation phrasing.** Qwen3.5-9B's milk case correctly avoided dairy and added a clarifying note — `"กะทิ (สูตรไม่มีนมวัว/ของแท้)"` ("no cow's milk") — but got wrongly blocked: `"Allergy violation: พบ 'นม' ในสูตร (ผู้ใช้แพ้ milk)"`, because `check_allergy`'s substring match found `"นม"` inside `"ไม่มีนมวัว"`. **Deliberately not fixed** — considered a negation-aware regex heuristic (e.g. `"ไม่มี" + allergen` → treat as safe) and rejected it: it risks introducing false *negatives* (missing a real violation sitting near unrelated negation text elsewhere in the same string), which is a worse failure direction for a safety check than the over-blocking it would fix. Logged as a known limitation instead, same treatment as the existing Thai Unicode-normalization entry.
  - The known-gap cases behaved exactly as predicted for both local models: `gluten_allergy_soy_sauce_known_gap` and `peanut_allergy_satay_sauce_known_gap` both passed `check_allergy()` undetected for Gemini and 9B (4B's gluten case separately failed on an unrelated `"Invalid instructions"` schema issue, not an allergy miss) — consistent, reproducible evidence for the planned Allergen Validation Knowledge Graph's motivation, not one-off noise.
  - Also newly visible at n=8: 4B's output quality degrades much more on recipe types outside the original 3 fixtures' base dishes (Larb Moo, Gaeng Kiew Wan Gai, Moo Satay) — noticeably more garbled English/nonsense-word leakage (`"Febru่"`, `"society"`, `"DLL"`, `"Portsmouth"`) than seen on the narrow set, even with the guided-JSON schema fix still in place (it constrains script, not word validity — consistent with the already-logged malformed-word finding).
  - Pod deleted immediately after both runs, confirmed via `list-pods` (empty). Total additional spend: ~$0.49 (under 1 GPU-hour).
- **Retest with the `ถั่ว`/soy fix in place (2026-09-19, fresh L4 pod `fhayrru97yp0d2`, `$0.49/hr`) — done specifically to get a corrected number set, not to hunt for new findings.**

  | Candidate | Before fix | After fix |
  |---|---|---|
  | Gemini (run paired w/ 4B) | 0.625 / 0.0 halluc | **0.875** / 0.0 halluc |
  | Gemini (run paired w/ 9B) | 0.875 / 0.125 | **0.875** / 0.0 halluc |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.625 / 0.75, 15.0s | 0.5 / 0.75, 17.0s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.375 / 0.75, 21.3s | **0.375** / 0.75, 18.1s (unchanged) |

  Read per-case, not just the aggregate: **Gemini improved cleanly on both runs** (the soy false positive on its peanut case is gone, verified by reading `reason` — both peanut cases now `null`/pass). **9B's numbers are identical before/after** — expected, since 9B's own peanut-case failure (`"พบ 'ถั่วลิสง' ในสูตร"`) was always a *real* violation (the model actually included peanuts), not the soy bug; its milk case still fails on the deliberately-unfixed negation false positive, exactly as predicted. **4B dropped further (0.625 → 0.5)** — not a regression from the fix, traced to two new-this-run `"Stir-fry without oil"` failures (an unrelated instruction-following issue, same failure text seen once before in the pre-fix run's Gemini/9B blocks) — a reminder that n=8 is still small enough for run-to-run variance in what specifically fails, even though the *fixed* bugs behaved exactly as expected. The known-gap cases (gluten, peanut-satay) passed undetected again on both models, in both runs — 4 for 4, reinforcing that this is a consistent pattern, not noise.
  - Pod deleted after, confirmed via `list-pods` (empty). Total additional spend: ~$0.49.
  - **This is now the current, corrected 8-case baseline.** Any future comparison (further fixes, fine-tuning evidence, latency-acceptance decision) should read from this retest, not the earlier pre-fix numbers above.

- **Lightweight prompt-only validation before committing to fine-tuning (2026-09-19/20, fresh L4 pod `chmrw7xyeu9nc6`, `$0.49/hr`) — done specifically to test whether the two "looks-trainable" failure categories (explicit-allergen inclusion, stir-fry-without-oil) respond to a cheap prompt fix before spending on real fine-tuning, per the DISSENT raised when the user proposed accepting worse latency in exchange for training-driven quality.** Added local-model-only rules 8-9 to `prompts.py` (commit `933a158c`) and re-ran the 8-case set.

  | Candidate | Before prompt fix | After prompt fix |
  |---|---|---|
  | Gemini (unaffected, control) | 0.875 / 0.0 halluc | 0.875 / 0.0 halluc |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.5 / 0.75, 17.0s | 0.75 / 0.625, 14.5s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.375 / 0.75, 18.1s | 0.625 / 1.0, 19.4s |

  **Read past the aggregate — the two models' improvements are not equally trustworthy:**
  - **4B's number is confounded, not a clean win.** Read the actual `reason`/recipe text: both new `"Stir-fry without oil"` fails had oil in the recipe, just misspelled (`"น้ำม้น"`, `"น้ำมื่อ"` instead of `"น้ำมัน"`) — a false negative from `check_logic`'s exact-substring match. The one shrimp-allergy case that newly passed did so because the model spelled `กะปิ` as `"กะperi"` and swapped in an entirely different, unrelated recipe — a false negative from `check_allergy`, not genuine allergen avoidance. **4B's Thai spelling quality was visibly worse this run than in prior runs** (garbled words even inside otherwise-correct sentences), which is enough on its own to move both check outcomes in either direction — the "improvement" cannot be attributed to rules 8-9 with this run alone.
  - **9B shows a genuine, verified partial win.** `peanut_allergy_should_avoid_peanut` and `egg_allergy_should_avoid_egg` both now `valid: true` with the actual allergen fully absent from `adjusted_ingredients` (not hidden by misspelling) — read directly, real behavior change. But two other cases got *worse in substance* while incidentally being caught: `peanut_allergy_satay_sauce_known_gap` explicitly wrote `"ถั่วลิสง"` and recommended it as a garnish for a peanut-allergic user (this run only passed the *check* by coincidentally spelling the allergen out; prior runs of the same "known gap" case evaded detection by *not* spelling it out — the case's outcome depends on incidental wording, not a stable underlying behavior). `milk_allergy...`'s new `"Stir-fry without oil"` failure is a *legitimate* `check_logic` catch this time (no oil at all in a coconut-milk curry that loosely used the word `"ผัด"`) but never reached `check_allergy` to check for real dairy — see the pipeline-ordering finding below.
  - **Net read: prompt-only rules had a real, partial, non-zero effect on 9B for the categories they targeted, but the "known gap" cases are not a stable metric signal (their pass/fail flips on incidental wording choices, independent of any actual improvement) and n=8 with single runs per condition is still too small/noisy to treat any of this as strong evidence for or against fine-tuning.**
  - **Separate, more actionable finding — not about the model at all:** `main.py`'s `validate_recipe()` 6-step pipeline stops at the first failing check. `check_logic` (step 3, a quality check) runs *before* `check_allergy` (step 5, a safety check) — so whenever a recipe fails step 3 for an unrelated reason (like the loose-`"ผัด"`-wording case above), nothing downstream is ever evaluated, meaning **a real allergy violation could be silently sitting in a recipe that already failed for a different reason, and the system never finds out.**
  - Pod deleted after both runs, confirmed via `list-pods` (empty). Total additional spend: ~$0.49.

- **Pipeline-ordering fix (2026-09-20, commit `ef865475`, confirmed `/backend/main.py` edit): moved `check_allergy` to run right after `check_ingredients`, ahead of `check_flavor`/`check_logic`/`check_nutrition`, in `validate_recipe()`'s 6-step pipeline.** No test asserted a specific ordering among the 6 stages (only that shape-validation runs before all of them), so the reorder was safe to make directly. Verified empirically, not just by test count: constructed a recipe with both a real dairy allergen (`ครีม`, milk allergy) and an unrelated logic failure (stir-fry instructions with no oil) — before this fix that would have reported `"Stir-fry without oil"` (masking the allergy issue entirely, per the finding above); after the fix it correctly reports the allergy violation. 148/148 backend tests passing. This is a deliberate behavior change to `/api/generate-recipe-text`'s live retry-feedback loop: when a recipe has multiple simultaneous problems, the LLM's next-attempt feedback now surfaces the safety issue first rather than whichever quality issue happened to be checked first.
  - **New gap surfaced while verifying this fix (flagged, not fixed — out of scope for this task):** `ALLERGEN_MAP`'s milk category blocks `"cheese"` (English) but not its common Thai transliteration `"ชีส"` — same class of gap as the `กะปิ` derived-ingredient issue, a different specific term. Logged in `MEMORY.md`.
  - **Fixed immediately after (2026-09-20, commit `1be328b5`, confirmed `/backend/main.py` edit): added `"ชีส"` to milk's triggers/blocks.** Verified directly: a recipe containing `ชีส` under a milk allergy now blocks. 148/148 tests passing.

- **Final retest with every fix from this session in place (`กะปิ`, `ถั่ว`/soy, pipeline-ordering, `ชีส`) — 2026-09-20, pod `y6xisx2eoo3gfd` (EUR-IS-1, `$0.49/hr` — two earlier attempts in `EU-RO-1`, `rud577sfrl0ash` and `7jv7c0sfgqc4h2`, sat with no `runtime` info for 2+ minutes each and were deleted without being billed for GPU time beyond provisioning; switching data center to `EUR-IS-1` via `dataCenterIds` resolved it immediately):**

  | Candidate | Original baseline | After `ถั่ว` fix | After prompt-only fix | **Final (all fixes)** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Gemini | 0.625–0.875 / 0.0–0.125 halluc | 0.875 / 0.0 | 0.875 / 0.0 | **0.875 / 0.0**, ~3.0s |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.625 / 0.75, 15.0s | 0.5 / 0.75, 17.0s | 0.75 / 0.625, 14.5s | **0.625 / 0.625**, 16.4s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.375 / 0.75, 21.3s | 0.375 / 0.75, 18.1s | 0.625 / 1.0, 19.4s | **0.75 / 0.875**, 19.2s |

  **9B improved monotonically across every fix round (0.375 → 0.375 → 0.625 → 0.75)** — its final run: `peanut_allergy_should_avoid_peanut` and `egg_allergy_should_avoid_egg` again genuinely omit the allergen (consistent with the prompt-fix run), the milk case passed this time (the deliberately-unfixed negation false positive didn't recur this run — consistent with it being a real but intermittent gap, not fixed, just not triggered here), `shrimp_allergy` still correctly caught (`กะปิ`), and the only failure was `gluten_allergy_soy_sauce_known_gap` — this time for an unrelated reason (`"Frying without oil"`), not a missed allergy — reinforcing that the known-gap cases are a noisy signal case-to-case, not a stable one.
  - **4B landed between its two prior extremes (0.5, 0.75) at 0.625** — consistent with the already-logged conclusion that 4B's schema-validity number this benchmark is dominated by run-to-run spelling-corruption noise, not a clean signal of any specific fix's effect.
  - **This is the current, final baseline for this session's local-LLM-generator work.** Any future decision (latency-acceptance, fine-tuning) should read from this table, not any of the earlier ones above.
  - Total additional spend this final round: ~$0.49 (the two stuck EU-RO-1 pods were deleted before any meaningful GPU-hour accrued). Verified via `runpodctl user`: account balance now **$6.9061764442** of the $10 top-up — total spend across all local-LLM-generator benchmarking (2026-09-18 through 2026-09-20) is **$3.09**, of which **~$1.29** was this session's work (starting balance $8.1957417273, confirmed at the top of this session).

- **Eval fixtures expanded again, 8 → 15 cases (2026-09-20, commit `34242326`), specifically to isolate whether ingredient-hallucination is driven by allergy-avoidance pressure or is a general model habit — the one large remaining gap between Gemini and the local models at n=8.** Added 5 no-allergy cases (no allergy pressure at all, to measure baseline hallucination rate) and 2 allergy cases pairing the allergen with a dish that traditionally contains it (`Tom Yum Goong` + shrimp allergy, `Som Tam` + peanut allergy — tests real substitution behavior, not incidental-ingredient avoidance). Also adds dish-type diversity (soup/ยำ/curry) beyond the previous set's stir-fry-heavy mix. 148/148 tests passing after the expansion.

- **Full n=15 benchmark run (2026-09-20, fresh L4 pod `c8t42t85rwqbm7`, `EUR-IS-1`, `$0.49/hr` — used `dataCenterIds` explicitly this time since two consecutive `EU-RO-1` pods had sat with no `runtime` populated for 2+ minutes each earlier this session and were deleted unused):**

  | Candidate | Schema validity | Hallucination rate | Mean latency |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Gemini | **0.933** | 0.0 | 2.2–2.6s |
  | Qwen3.5-4B | 0.6 | 0.733 | 14.8s |
  | Qwen3.5-9B (fp8) | 0.733 | 0.8 | 19.2s |

  Validity/hallucination numbers are consistent with the n=8 final baseline (9B: 0.75/0.875 → 0.733/0.8; 4B still the noisiest of the three, as already established) — the larger n didn't overturn anything, it sharpened the picture.

  **Key finding this round, read directly from the 5 no-allergy cases (no allergy-avoidance motive present at all): both models hallucinated in 4 of 5 of them** (`no_allergy_single_ingredient_baseline`, `no_allergy_many_ingredients_baseline`, and either `no_allergy_soup_dish_type_diversity` or `no_allergy_yum_salad_dish_type_diversity` depending on the model, plus `no_allergy_curry_dish_type_diversity`) — **identical 4/5 rate for both 4B and 9B.** This directly answers the question the expansion was built to answer: ingredient hallucination is **not** primarily an artifact of the model trying to substitute away from a stated allergen — it happens just as often with zero allergy pressure. It's a general instruction-following gap (not reliably respecting the prompt's "use only the given ingredients + the 7 named staples" rule), the same category of failure prompt rule 9 partially improved for explicit-allergen cases in the earlier lightweight validation.
  - **This sharpens the case for a narrowly-scoped fine-tuning target, if training is pursued: reduce ingredient-hallucination specifically, framed as improving the local generator's instruction-following reliability (fits within the existing Critic-Feedback Loop / Allergen Validation contribution framing — not a new 4th contribution) rather than "make the local model better than Gemini overall."** Latency (Gemini ~2.4s vs. local 15–19s) remains unaffected by any of this and is a separate, still-unresolved acceptance question.
  - Pod deleted after both runs, confirmed via `list-pods` (empty). Additional spend this round: **~$0.23** (verified via `runpodctl user`: balance $6.9061764442 → $6.6755226636). Running total: **$3.32** of the $10 top-up.

**Local LLM Fine-Tuning — dataset-construction pipeline (2026-09-21, worktree `worktree-local-llm-finetune`, 6-task SDD plan, all tasks done, 1 final-review fix wave, merged to `main` local — same "Critic-Feedback Loop / Allergen Validation instrumentation" framing as the generator work above, still not a 4th contribution):**
- Advisor's training-component requirement now has enough evidence to act on (per the n=15 finding above: hallucination is a general instruction-following gap, not allergy-avoidance-driven — the one clearly-scoped, defensible fine-tuning target). Full evidence trail written up as a standalone reference doc for the advisor: [`docs/LOCAL_LLM_EVIDENCE_LOG.md`](docs/LOCAL_LLM_EVIDENCE_LOG.md) (gitignored `docs/`, not tracked — local reference only, per explicit user request not to track it).
- Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-21-local-llm-finetune-design.md`; Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-21-local-llm-finetune.md` (both gitignored `docs/`, present in this checkout only).
- **Advisor review before writing the plan caught 4 real gaps in the initial design**, all resolved before implementation: (1) eval-contamination risk — training on a dish that's also an eval fixture's `base_recipe` would measure memorization, not generalization; resolved with a dish-family exclusion matcher, verified to catch both real near-duplicates found by direct file inspection (`ส้มตำแตงร้าน`/Som Tam, `ยำปลาหมึกสด`/Stir-Fried Squid). (2) `nutrition` field has no deterministic source — `IngredientNutrition` table exists in the schema but has zero rows and no code path uses it (confirmed by direct check, not assumed); resolved by masking `nutrition` from the training loss entirely, not fabricating values. (3) Naive "drop 1-2 ingredients from a real recipe" sampling never produces the sparse-input shape (1-2 given vs. a full dish) where hallucination actually occurs; resolved with a two-tier design — Tier A (near-complete, full supervision) + Tier B (sparse, ingredients-only supervision, ≥50% of the dataset by design). (4) 4B vs. 9B model choice — picked 9B because its benchmark signal has been reliably monotonic all session, while 4B's is confounded by spelling noise (already logged in MEMORY.md); RTX 4090 picked over cheaper L4 for training infra (HIGH vs. LOW RunPod availability, user confirmed the price trade-off explicitly per the standing GPU-cost-confirmation rule).
- **What was built (`backend/finetune/`, new package, 6 tasks, TDD throughout):** a Thai-text combining-mark normalization fix (corrects a previously-wrong MEMORY.md hypothesis — plain `unicodedata.normalize("NFC", ...)` does NOT fix the bug; the real fix decomposes SARA AM and reorders tone marks, verified directly before shipping); Thai ingredient-name extraction + bilingual dish-family matching helpers; a recipe-source loader for the `pythainlp/thai_food_v1.0` xlsx dataset (146/159 rows usable, with markdown-fallback parsing for rows missing structured columns); the eval-fixture exclusion matcher; Tier A/Tier B SFT example builders with **real prompt parity** to `LocalLLMGenerator` (imports `build_recipe_prompt(..., include_example=True)` directly, not a reimplementation); and the final dataset-assembly script enforcing the Tier B ≥50% floor in code (capping Tier A, never fabricating Tier B).
- **Real-data verification caught 2 more implementation-level bugs no synthetic test could have found** (Task 2's implementer, then Task 5's): (a) the original `dish_families_overlap` design used single-language (Thai-only) keyword matching, which can never match against the eval fixtures' English/transliterated names (`"Som Tam"` etc.) — the plan was corrected mid-implementation to bilingual `(thai_form, english_form)` pairs, checked in either name order. (b) Task 5's implementer verified the instruction-clause-splitting heuristic against all 146 real recipes (not just the plan's one synthetic test) and found it would have shredded real instructions into single words/digits via an unsafe fallback, and separately leaked a dropped ingredient's name into 46/217 (21%) of real Tier A examples — fixed by broadening Thai clause-connector boundaries and having the splitter return `None` (masked from the loss, same contract as Tier B) whenever a leak-free split can't be guaranteed, rather than ever shipping unsafe training data.
- **Final whole-branch review (opus) ran the real pipeline end-to-end and the full test suite independently** (not just read the diff) — confirmed all cross-task interfaces line up, `nutrition` is genuinely never touched, and both real eval-fixture near-duplicates are excluded. Caught 2 Important findings, both documentation gaps rather than code defects: (1) the target-sequence/loss-mask construction for the actual training step was never written down, despite determining whether the eventual fine-tune passes its own schema-validity regression guard — now documented as design spec §4.6, an explicit open item to resolve before training (recommending fabricate-and-mask placeholder values for the 5 unsupervised schema fields as the safer default, not decided as final). (2) The exclusion list itself was never recorded anywhere auditable, despite being an explicit spec §3.1 requirement — fixed, both in the design spec and by having `build_dataset.py` print the excluded names. Also fixed in the same wave: a `ZeroDivisionError` guard, deduplication (4/473 real examples were exact duplicates from a genuinely duplicated source row), and a cross-reference comment noting the pantry-staple list now exists as 3 independently-synced copies across the codebase. One residual Minor parked (not blocking): the dedup step runs after the Tier B floor cap rather than before, so the floor is only pre-dedup-guaranteed — immaterial at the real duplicate rate (0.8%), flagged for a future re-run at larger scale.
- **Real-run output (2026-09-21, `pythainlp/thai_food_v1.0` xlsx + the 15-case eval fixtures): 469 training examples after dedup (200 Tier A, 269 Tier B, 57.4% Tier B — floor satisfied), 10 recipes excluded for eval-fixture overlap.** Dataset written to `finetune_dataset.jsonl` (gitignored, not committed — generated artifact).
- 182/182 backend tests passing on `main` after merge (148 pre-existing + 5 from `eval_metrics`/Task 1 + 29 new across the `finetune/` package's own test files).
- **§4.6 resolved (user-confirmed, 2026-09-21): option (a)** — the 5 unsupervised schema fields (`recipe_name`, `servings`, `diet_tags`, `safety_warning`, `nutrition`) get fixed placeholder values in the training completion with their tokens loss-masked, keeping the completion's JSON shape consistent with what `LocalLLMGenerator`/`validate_recipe` expect without fabricating real data.

**Post-Plan Operational Runbook — QLoRA training + benchmark, RunPod RTX 4090, 2026-09-21 (pod `3p5u3jn1fceqsv`, $0.74/hr, deleted after use, ~$2.2 total spend this round):**
- **Training ran clean:** `train.py` (local-scratch-only, not committed — pod-only tooling, no `backend/` dependency, matches this session's established pattern) trained 3 epochs on the 469-example dataset, QLoRA (nf4 4-bit base + LoRA r=16 on all 32/32 decoder layers' `q/k/v/o_proj` + `gate/up/down_proj`, 29.1M trainable params / 0.32%). Loss 1.74 → 0.357 (final `train_loss` 0.82 avg), `mean_token_accuracy` 0.944, zero-supervised-token guard passed (0/469). Runtime 4413s (~74 min), matched estimate.
- **Critical infra finding (new, logged to MEMORY.md): vLLM 0.29.0 silently serves the base model instead of a registered LoRA adapter on this Qwen3.5-9B hybrid (GDN/linear-attention) architecture — no error, `Loaded new LoRA adapter` logs normally, but output is byte-identical to base at `temperature=0`, reproduced across fp8 and bf16 serving and with prefix caching both on and off.** Caught before trusting any benchmark number from it — the first 15-case benchmark run through vLLM (schema validity 0.733→0.6, hallucination 0.733→0.867) was **comparing the base model against itself** and is invalid; discarded, never used for any conclusion.
- **Adapter verified genuinely real and working**, isolating the vLLM serving bug from a training defect: full LoRA module coverage (128 `lora_A` modules across all 32/32 layers), non-zero `lora_B` weights (~0.006–0.008 magnitude, not still-zero-init), and a same-process `model.disable_adapter()` toggle test (identical weights, only the adapter flag differs) produced different, adapter-shaped output — a held-out Tier B prompt (single given ingredient) generated a near-minimal ingredient list with the adapter on, vs. a long unfocused response with it off.
- **Real benchmark: direct `transformers`+`peft` generation on the pod (bypassing vLLM entirely)**, same process/weights, adapter toggled via `disable_adapter()`, identical chat template (`enable_thinking=False`) and `temperature=1.0`, **n=3 repeats × 15 fixture cases × 2 arms (90 generations, n=45 pooled per arm)** — a genuinely prompt- and precision-matched comparison:
  - **Real, verified improvement:** among successfully-parsed recipes, ingredient hallucination went from 34/44 (77.3%) to **0/33 (0%)**. Checked this wasn't a degenerate/empty-output artifact — a per-case ingredient-coverage check confirmed the fine-tuned model still includes the user's actually-given ingredients (2/2, 5/5, 3/3 in the cases checked), it's just no longer padding with invented extras. The mean `adjusted_ingredients` length dropping (7.68 → 2.79) reflects the eval set's own mostly-sparse (2-ingredient) case shape matching Tier B's trained target, not ingredient loss.
  - **Real, verified regression — allergy-avoidance got worse, reproducibly:** counting by distinct case with per-case repeat consistency (not pooled generation count, which overstates spread): baseline had 1 case failing 3/3 repeats (`shrimp_allergy_should_avoid_shrimp_and_shrimp_paste`); the fine-tuned model had **3 cases failing 3/3** — the same shrimp case, plus `peanut_allergy_should_avoid_peanut` (was only 1/3 in baseline) and `shrimp_allergy_tom_yum_dish_type_diversity` (was **0/3 in baseline** — a previously-clean case that now fails every time). This is a reproducible safety regression, not noise.
  - **Unresolved, explicitly not attributed:** JSON parse-failure rate rose sharply (baseline 1/45, finetuned 12/45). Two plausible, un-separated causes in this data: `max_new_tokens=800` truncation (finetuned mean latency 34.3s vs. baseline 25.4s — longer generations more likely to hit the cap; several failures are literal mid-string truncation) vs. a real generation-quality gap from §4.6's masking design (the model was never given loss signal on how to *generate* the 5 masked fields itself, only ever saw them pre-filled during training). Raw generated text was not retained before the pod was deleted, so the actual cause cannot be checked post hoc — logged as an open question, not resolved as either explanation.
- **Verdict: this adapter should not ship.** It demonstrably fixes the one thing it was scoped to fix (ingredient hallucination, verified non-degenerate), but at the cost of a reproducible allergy-safety regression that outweighs it for a food-safety-relevant feature. Net negative for production use as-is; retains value as a proof-of-concept that the Tier A/B dataset design and training mechanics work as intended — the failure is in what the training signal didn't cover (allergy-avoidance wasn't part of the loss-supervised target at all), not in the SFT pipeline itself.
- **The trained adapter itself was not saved anywhere** — it existed only at `/root/qwen35-9b-hallu-lora` on the now-deleted pod, never copied to this machine. Reproducible from scratch (`finetune_dataset.jsonl` is committed-adjacent/gitignored but present locally, `train.py` exists in this session's local scratchpad only) but would cost another full training run (~74 min GPU time) to regenerate — training is not bit-for-bit deterministic, so a re-run would not be guaranteed to reproduce this exact adapter.
- All quantitative claims above are from `direct_finetune_benchmark_result.json` (local scratchpad, not committed — reproducible artifact, not source data). The invalid vLLM-based first benchmark run and its numbers do not appear in this entry and must not be cited elsewhere.
- **Decision: stop pursuing local-model fine-tuning further.** Frame this whole effort as **"demonstrated, not shipped"** — same treatment this spec already gives Personalization and Expiry Tracking above, for the same reason (a real result exists and is honestly reported, but it doesn't clear the bar to ship or to carry a production KPI). The advisor's requirement was "a training component with sufficient academic scope" — this dataset-construction pipeline + real QLoRA training run + honestly-reported win/regression result already satisfies that; the hallucination reduction is sufficient proof-of-concept on its own. The allergy regression is a finding to report, not a defect to chase — fine-tuning was never one of the 3 core contributions (nutrition engine / allergen validation / critic-feedback loop) and re-training to fix it would be scope creep into a 4th contribution the project never committed to. No further RunPod/GPU spend planned on this track.

**Follow-up (2026-09-21, same day): does the shipped critic-feedback loop (contribution #3) recover from allergy violations? Tested against real Gemini (production model), not the deleted fine-tuned local adapter — chosen because the question "does retry-with-feedback fix this class of failure at all" is answerable on the real shipped path without any GPU cost, at the cost of not telling us anything about the local model specifically.**
- Ran the real `main.py` retry logic (`call_agentic_llm`/`validate_recipe`, imported read-only, replicated call-for-call — not a reimplementation) against the 3 "known_gap" allergy fixture cases (the ones specifically curated as most likely to trip a zero-shot violation), up to 3 attempts each, real billed Gemini calls.
- 2 of 3 cases passed on attempt 1 — Gemini didn't violate zero-shot, so there was nothing for the retry loop to demonstrate on those.
- **The 3rd case (`shrimp_allergy_should_avoid_shrimp_and_shrimp_paste`) failed all 3 attempts and returned a hard error to the user — but the underlying food became allergen-safe by attempt 3.** Attempt 1: real violation (`กะปิ`/shrimp paste present, flagged via a hedge note containing `'กุ้ง'`). Attempt 2: model removed the hedge note but kept `กะปิ` — correctly re-flagged on `'กะปิ'` itself. Attempt 3: model swapped `กะปิ` for `เต้าเจี้ยว` (fermented soybean paste — genuinely allergen-free), but its own substitution-rationale note ("used instead of shrimp paste to avoid shrimp") reintroduced the literal word `'กุ้ง'`, triggering the already-known negation-blind substring false-positive (see MEMORY.md) and burning the last retry on food that was already correct.
- **Reading:** the critic-feedback loop mechanism itself worked as designed — it adapted each round and correctly caught 2 real problems in a row. What defeated it was the allergy checker's negation-blind substring matching interacting with the *feedback message's own weakness* (a raw `"found 'X'"` string tells the model a word appeared, not whether it was in a real ingredient vs. its own explanatory prose) — the model kept writing safety-conscious notes to explain its fix, and those notes kept re-triggering the same class of false positive. Not a failure of the retry-with-reason design; a failure of what it's retrying against.
- **Not fixed** — same call as the original negation-false-positive entry: any fix (e.g. separating ingredient name from free-text notes in the schema, or making the retry feedback name the specific ingredient/field rather than the matched word) is a real, non-trivial design change to `backend/main.py`'s validation/feedback contract, out of scope for this follow-up check. Logged as a concrete, reproduced cost of the known limitation, not a new problem to fix today.
- Script: local scratchpad only (`test_critic_loop_gemini.py`), not committed. Result: `backend/critic_loop_gemini_result.json` (gitignored, not committed — same generated-artifact pattern as the fine-tuning benchmark results above).

**Auth Phase 3 — real backend-verified OTP password reset, replacing the fully-mocked flow (2026-09-12, worktree `auth-phase3-otp-reset` branch `worktree-auth-phase3-otp-reset`, 8-task SDD plan, all tasks done, 1 final-review fix wave) — product envelope (Auth), ไม่ใช่ contribution หลักที่ต้องมี evaluation:**
- Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-12-auth-phase3-otp-reset-design.md` (local/ignored — `docs/` is gitignored, not present in this worktree checkout, only in the main checkout); Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-12-auth-phase3-otp-reset.md`; ledger: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-12-auth-phase3-otp-reset/progress.md`
- **What was built:** `requestOtp`/`resendOtp`/`verifyOtp`/`resetPassword` in `AuthContext.jsx` were fully client-side mocks before this phase (no backend OTP code existed at all). Now real end-to-end: new `password_reset_otps` table + `backend/otp.py` (generation/hashing/verification helpers) + three real endpoints — `POST /api/auth/forgot-password`, `POST /api/auth/verify-otp`, `POST /api/auth/reset-password` — plus real SMTP delivery in `backend/email_sender.py` (added 2026-10-04; the earlier logging-only correction in this history is superseded).
- **Real password-reset email delivery (2026-10-04):** `EMAIL_BACKEND` is required and must be explicitly `console` (local development; logs recipient + OTP) or `smtp` (real delivery; never logs either and never falls back). SMTP uses stdlib `smtplib` + `EmailMessage`, configurable host/port, STARTTLS with the default verified SSL context, authentication, a 10-second timeout, and the approved concise Thai subject/body. The Gmail capstone account uses port 587 + an App Password stored only in `backend/.env`; no package was added. FastAPI lifespan validates config at startup. `forgot-password` runs the blocking sender through the existing `run_in_threadpool`, commits only after **Delivery Accepted**, does not retry ambiguous failures, and returns the same generic 200 after delivery or commit failure while rolling back and logging only `user_id` + exception class. The known timing difference and client-side 60-second wait after silent delivery failure remain accepted limitations; per-IP rate limiting, queue/workers, HTML templates, bounce webhooks and provider monitoring remain backlog/out of scope. Domain language is recorded in `GLOSSARY.md`; transaction rationale is in `docs/adr/0001-commit-password-reset-otp-after-delivery-acceptance.md` (local/ignored).
- **Verification (2026-10-04 email-delivery update; local/live follow-up 2026-10-05):** `test_email_sender.py` 8/8 passed; `test_password_reset_endpoints.py` 23/23 passed; guarded full backend suite passed **672 tests, skipped=2, external network violations=0, exit 0** after loading local non-provider secrets, clearing only `GEMINI_API_KEY`, selecting `EMAIL_BACKEND=console`, and blocking external sockets. `scripts/setup-gmail-smtp.sh` provides idempotent, hidden-input local credential setup; its shared wizard library matches the source template line-for-line and `bash -n` passes (`shellcheck` is not installed, so it was not run and no package was added). The user ran the wizard and the resulting SMTP configuration passed `validate_email_config()` offline without printing either credential. The wizard probes its Python candidates before validation; an outside-sandbox check confirmed that both the user-level base executable and `backend/venv` run Python 3.12.10, so an earlier “removed Python” diagnosis was a sandbox visibility error rather than a broken venv. After exact user authorization, one direct Gmail SMTP smoke message was attempted once (no retry, no database row, random OTP not printed) and returned `SMTP_SEND_ACCEPTED=YES`; the user then confirmed that the message and matching code arrived in the recipient Inbox. The exact code is intentionally not persisted in project files. This verifies the direct sender path through Inbox receipt; the endpoint/DB/browser flow remains a separate check.
- **Key security properties:** OTP codes are hashed with **HMAC-SHA256 keyed by `OTP_HMAC_SECRET`** (`otp.hash_otp_code`), not plain SHA-256 — the 6-digit keyspace (1,000,000 values) is small enough to brute-force offline from a leaked hash, so a keyed hash is load-bearing, not decorative (`test_hash_otp_code_is_deterministic_and_keyed` now actually asserts this against an unkeyed SHA-256 comparison). Reset tickets are plain SHA-256 (safe — high-entropy `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` token, not a small keyspace). All state transitions (`create_otp_for_user`, `attempt_verify_otp`, `consume_reset_ticket`) use **atomic compare-and-set `UPDATE ... WHERE ... RETURNING`**, not read-then-write checks, so they're race-safe under concurrent requests. `forgot-password` and `verify-otp` return **identical generic responses** for known/unknown emails and right/wrong codes (non-enumeration). `reset-password` does the ticket-consume + password-hash-update + all-refresh-tokens-revoke in **one transaction** (single commit) so a failure partway through can never leave a burned ticket with the old password still active, or a new password with old sessions still valid.
- **Final whole-branch review (opus, range `c721f67b..d5643cc6`) caught and fixed 1 Critical + 3 Important before merge** — the exact kind of composability bug per-task review can't see (each task only reviews its own diff):
  1. **Critical, fixed in this wave: the 5-attempt cap was bypassable via resend, defeating OTP brute-force protection entirely.** `otp.get_active_otp_created_within`'s cooldown-check predicate required `consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now` — but the 5th-wrong-attempt path in `attempt_verify_otp` deliberately sets `expires_at = now` to invalidate the row (that invalidation itself is correct and unchanged). Consequence: once a row is invalidated by hitting the attempt cap, it became invisible to the cooldown check, so an immediate `forgot-password` resend issued a brand-new OTP with `attempts` reset to 0 and no cooldown delay — attacker loop: request OTP → 5 wrong guesses → immediate resend (bypasses cooldown) → repeat, no time bound on guessing at all. Fixed by changing the predicate to depend **only on `created_at`** (renamed `get_active_otp_created_within` → `get_recent_otp_request` since "active" no longer describes what it checks — see the function's docstring in `backend/otp.py`). Pinned with a new regression test, `test_attempt_cap_bypass_via_resend_is_blocked` (`backend/tests/test_password_reset_endpoints.py`) — empirically confirmed it fails against the old predicate (2 rows created instead of 1) and passes against the fix, by temporarily reverting the predicate, re-running the test, and restoring the fix.
  2. Important, fixed: `OTP_HMAC_SECRET` was a new required env var completely undocumented in `README.md` — backend wouldn't boot for anyone pulling `main` without already knowing this secret was required. Added, matching the exact format/hint style of the existing `JWT_SECRET_KEY` entry.
  3. Important, fixed: `backend/otp.py` never called `load_dotenv()` itself — worked only by import-order luck (`main.py` imports `auth`, which calls `load_dotenv()`, before it imports `otp`). Any other entry point importing `otp` directly would have crashed. Fixed by adding the same `from dotenv import load_dotenv` / `load_dotenv()` pattern `auth.py` uses.
  4. Important, fixed: the plan's own Testing section named "ticket expiry" and "cross-user ticket rejection" as required coverage; neither existed. Added `test_consume_reset_ticket_fails_when_ticket_expired` (DB-level, `test_otp_db.py`, backdates `ticket_expires_at` directly) and `test_reset_ticket_resolves_only_to_its_own_user_not_another` (endpoint-level, `test_password_reset_endpoints.py`, two registered users, confirms user A's ticket resets only A's password — the load-bearing assertion is that user B's login with B's original, untouched password still succeeds afterward; the accompanying "B's login with A's new password fails" check is trivially true by construction and only rounds out the picture). A third named test, "concurrent-verify race safety" (real multi-threaded), stays explicitly deferred — flagged with a one-line comment in `test_otp_db.py` (genuinely hard to force under SQLite+StaticPool's single shared connection) rather than silently missing.
  - Bundled cheap fixes from the same review's ledger triage: unused `PasswordResetOtp` import removed from `backend/main.py`; unused `import time` removed from `backend/tests/test_otp_db.py`; `frontend/src/pages/auth/OtpVerify.jsx`'s `handleResend` now captures `resendOtp()`'s `{success, error}` result and surfaces a failure via the existing `setError` state instead of silently starting the 60s cooldown lockout on a failed network call.
  - **Not done in this wave (out of the fix-wave's given scope, flagged for the user):** the ledger's final-review note also asked to update `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-12-auth-phase3-otp-reset-design.md`'s cooldown-predicate description to match the fix — `docs/` is gitignored and not present in this worktree checkout (only in the main checkout), so this file could not be reached from here; needs a manual edit in the main checkout.
- **Verified:** full backend suite `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` → **121/121 passing** (117 pre-existing at start of this wave + 4 new: `test_attempt_cap_bypass_via_resend_is_blocked`, `test_get_recent_otp_request_still_blocks_after_attempt_cap_invalidation`, `test_consume_reset_ticket_fails_when_ticket_expired`, `test_reset_ticket_resolves_only_to_its_own_user_not_another`; plus 1 pre-existing test strengthened with an extra assertion — keyed-hash-vs-plain-SHA256 — and 1 pre-existing test renamed to match the renamed function, neither changing the count); `bun run build` → exit 0, 2190 modules; `bun run lint` → same 9 pre-existing errors (`AddIngredient.jsx`, `CookingPage.jsx`, `RecipeDetail.jsx`, `pages_v2/*` — files this branch never touches), zero new.
- Files touched this wave: `backend/otp.py`, `backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/{test_otp_db.py,test_otp_crypto.py,test_password_reset_endpoints.py}`, `README.md`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/OtpVerify.jsx`, `spec.md` (this entry).
- **งานค้าง (backlog) update:** Auth Phase 1's and Phase 2's historical entries below still describe Phase 3 as not done; both backend OTP/reset logic and SMTP delivery are now implemented. Gmail accepted the separately authorized one-message sender smoke test on 2026-10-05 and the user confirmed Inbox receipt with the matching code. Remaining optional end-to-end verification is a full manual browser click-through against an endpoint-issued code.

**Auth Phase 2 — migrate `/api/user-ingredients` off the dev-user bridge (2026-09-08, worktree `auth-phase2` branch `worktree-auth-phase2`, bounded task, 1 review round) — product envelope (Auth), ไม่ใช่ contribution หลักที่ต้องมี evaluation:**
- Scope: only `/api/user-ingredients` (`GET`/`POST`/`DELETE`) — the only remaining endpoint still using `get_dev_user_id`. `/api/recipes` and the ingredient-image endpoints were already correctly public (no user data); `/api/user-preferences` was already built on real auth from day one (User Preferences entry below).
- `backend/main.py`: all 3 routes now take `current_user: User = Depends(get_current_user)` and scope every DB call to `current_user.id`; `POST`/`DELETE` (mutating) gained `Depends(verify_same_origin)` (CSRF), `GET` correctly left ungated (safe method). `get_dev_user_id`/`DEV_USER_EMAIL` fully removed from `main.py` — no dangling references.
- New `backend/tests/test_user_ingredients_endpoints.py` (8 tests) proves the actual point of the migration: two different registered users don't see or can't delete each other's rows — coverage Phase 1's own auth tests never exercised. 401/403 cases covered per route.

Independent review (sonnet): 0 Critical, 0 Important, 2 Minor (one fixed same-branch — missing DELETE-401 test; one deferred — `backend/database/seed_postgres.py`'s docstring still references the now-removed `DEV_USER_EMAIL` bridge, out of scope, flagged for manual cleanup per Hard Rule).
